⏺ Typecheck passes. Here's what was going on and what I changed.

  Why you got that macOS popup instead of WhatsApp

  That popup is the share step — it's the macOS share sheet. The difference
  between the two buttons:

  - "Open WhatsApp (link)" uses wa.me, which opens WhatsApp directly — but it
  can only carry text (the bill link), never a file.
  - "Share PDF file" used the browser's file-share, which on a computer always
  hands the PDF to macOS, and macOS decides which apps show up. On your Mac,
  WhatsApp isn't registered as a file-share target, so it simply isn't in that
  list (AirDrop/Messages/Notes are). That's an Apple/WhatsApp-desktop limitation
  — the web app can't force WhatsApp into that sheet.

  On a phone, that same button works the way you expected: WhatsApp appears in
  the sheet with the PDF already attached, one tap to send.

  What I changed

  Instead of leaving you stuck in a WhatsApp-less share sheet on desktop, the
  button now behaves per device:

  - On your Mac — the button is now "Send PDF on WhatsApp". It downloads the 
  bill PDF and opens WhatsApp Web with the short amount message prefilled (Hi 
  Rushikesh, Amount, Paid, Pending). You just drag the downloaded PDF into the 
  chat (or 📎 → Document) and hit send. No more dead-end share sheet.
  - On a phone — the button is "Share PDF to WhatsApp" and does the real one-tap
  native share with the PDF attached.

  The dialog text and the hint under the button now say exactly this, so it's
  clear which flow you're in.

  The honest tradeoff

  There is no way for any website to silently push a file straight into WhatsApp
  on a desktop — WhatsApp Web doesn't accept files from a URL or the share API.
  Your three real options:

  1. This new desktop flow — download + WhatsApp opens + drag the file in (one
  extra drag). ✅ built
  2. Use your phone for billing — true one-tap PDF-to-WhatsApp. ✅ built
  3. WhatsApp Business Cloud API — the server sends the PDF automatically, zero
  clicks, works from desktop too. This is the one you asked me to revert ("I
  have something in my mind").

  Want me to refresh the page so you can try the new desktop flow, or is option
  3 (auto-send via Business API) the "something in mind" you'd like to pursue?

⏺ All 17 tests pass. Done.

  What happens now when you click "Send PDF on WhatsApp" (on your Mac)

  1. The bill PDF downloads to your computer.
  2. WhatsApp opens directly to the customer's chat (e.g. Rushikesh's).
  3. The message box is prefilled with only the amount summary — no link:
  ▎ Hi Rushikesh,
  ▎ MNM Fashion — Invoice INV-000016
  ▎ Amount: Rs. …
  ▎ Paid: Rs. …
  ▎ Pending: Rs. …
  4. You drag the downloaded PDF into the chat (or 📎 → Document) and hit send.

  No more link, and no more dead-end macOS share sheet. The only manual step
  left on desktop is dragging the file in — that one step genuinely can't be
  automated by a website (WhatsApp Web won't let a browser attach a file for
  you). On a phone it's fully one-tap.

  To try it: refresh the browser tab (the frontend changed), and make sure your
  backend dev server reloaded (it serves the new no-link message). If it's
  running under npm run dev with watch, it already picked it up.

  If you want the message to say something different alongside the PDF — or
  nothing at all — tell me the exact wording and I'll set it.

⏺ Set. The desktop flow is now locked in as you chose, and more reliable. Here's
  exactly how it works:

  Sending a bill PDF on your Mac — 3 steps:
  1. Click "Send PDF on WhatsApp". The PDF downloads and WhatsApp Web opens
  straight to the customer's chat with the amount message already filled in.
  2. Drag the downloaded PDF into the chat. Easiest way: drag it right from
  Chrome's download bar at the bottom (no need to open Finder). Or click the 📎 
  → Document in WhatsApp and pick it from Downloads.
  3. Hit send.

  The amount message (Hi {name}, Amount, Paid, Pending) is already there, so the
  PDF lands with the summary.

  Why the drag step can't be skipped on desktop: WhatsApp Web deliberately
  blocks websites from attaching files to a chat — only you can, by hand. No
  website can get around that. The one way to skip it entirely is your phone:
  open this app on your phone, tap "Share PDF to WhatsApp", pick WhatsApp, and
  the PDF is attached automatically — one tap.

  Changes are live after you refresh (restart the dev server first if you
  haven't, since the dropdown packages were added last round). If the manual
  drag ever becomes a daily hassle, the WhatsApp Business API is the only true
  auto-send — just say the word and I'll build it.

⏺ Done. The New Bill page now has a prominent Scan tag box at the top that reads
  tags exactly like the one in your photo. It works three ways:

  1. USB / Bluetooth handheld scanner (the shop-counter way) — the scan box is
  auto-focused, so you just point the scanner at the tag and pull the trigger.
  It types the code and the item drops into the bill automatically. Scan the
  next tag, and the next — the box clears and re-focuses after each one.
  2. Type the item code + Enter — same result (handy if a barcode is torn).
  3. Camera button — opens the camera (phone or laptop webcam), point it at the
  barcode, and it adds the item.

  How it maps to your tag: the barcode encodes the item code 200144005; the app
  looks that up and adds the exact match — 132 · Zig-Zag Lahenga, Dark / 40 at
  its price — straight to the cart (shown in the preview above).

  Smart bits:
  - Scanning the same tag twice bumps the quantity to 2, 3, … (one scan per
  piece).
  - An out-of-stock tag is blocked with a warning instead of adding.
  - "Add product" (browse/search) is still there below for anything without a
  tag.

  Two things to know:
  - For a handheld USB scanner, make sure it's set to send Enter after the code
  — that's the factory default on virtually all of them, so it usually just
  works out of the box.
  - Camera scanning needs HTTPS + camera permission; if the camera is blocked,
  the scanner dialog gives you a type-it-in fallback.

  Refresh (restart the dev server if you haven't since the dropdown packages
  were added) and try scanning a tag. Want me to also add the scan box to the
  Stock Lookup page so staff can scan a tag to check price/stock without
  billing?

      185 +/** Distinct sizes a design is sold in, as a "20-24-28-32-36" 
          +run (numeric sizes sorted). */                                
      186 +function formatSizeGroup(sizes: string[]): string {           
      187 +  const distinct = Array.from(new Set(sizes.map((s) => s.trim(
          +)).filter(Boolean)));                                         
      188 +  if (distinct.length && distinct.every((s) => /^\d+$/.test(s)
          +)) {                                                          
      189 +    distinct.sort((a, b) => Number(a) - Number(b));           
      190 +  }                                                           
      191 +  return distinct.join('-');                                  
      192 +}                                                             
      193 +

            string }[]) {

✻ Brewed for 2m 41s

❯ what if year change then it'll change                                         

⏺ Yes — that's exactly how it's designed to work. The first 4 digits are the
  year pair and they roll over automatically based on the date, with no action
  from you. Only that year pair changes; 1008, the price, and 23 stay fixed.

  It reads the date at the moment a label is printed, so:

  ┌────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┐
  │ When you print │         Code for ₹1200          │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2026           │ 26271008120023 → 26271008120023 │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2027           │ 27281008120023 → 27281008120023 │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2028           │ 28291008120023 → 28291008120023 │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2099           │ 99001008120023 → 99001008120023 │
  └────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────┘

  A few things worth knowing:
  - It follows your computer/server's date, so the rollover happens on its own
  each January 1.
  - Already-printed tags don't change — they're paper. Only newly printed tags
  use the new year.
  - If you ever want to freeze the year (stop it from rolling), set
  PRICE_CODE_YEAR=2026 in .env and it'll always use that year until you change
  it. Right now it's not set, so it auto-rolls — which matches "26 = current
  year."

  So you don't need to do anything each year; it just updates itself. Want me to
  leave it auto-rolling (recommended), or pin it to a fixed year?

  28271008120023. Right now my code rolls both pairs (2627 → 2728), which isn't
  what you want. Let me fix it: drop the "next year" pair and make 271008 the
  fixed middle.

      56 +  PRICE_CODE_MID: z.string().default('271008'),

      58    PRICE_CODE_PAD: z.coerce.number().int().min(0).max(8).default
          (4),
      59    // Optional: pin the year (2-or-4 digit) instead of using the
           current year.

❯ for code 26271008{price}27 , here We got an feedback from custmore like they  
  are not getting understand what exactly price, they are getting confused      
  about price so can you please suggest me what we can do change also here i    
  want to add two price b2b and b2c both in the code so please suggest some     

⏺ Good feedback to act on. The root cause is simple: the coded number is 
  designed to hide the price, so a customer physically can't read it. A code can
  never double as the customer's price — to stop the confusion, the tag needs
  at least one plain, readable price.

  For B2B + B2C, the natural split is:
  - B2C (retail) — what a walk-in customer pays → should be plain and readable
  (this fixes the confusion).
  - B2B (wholesale) — business/bulk price → keep it coded (hidden from retail
  customers, staff decode it).

  So "both prices in the code area" works best as: one shown, one coded.
  Alongside whichever option you pick, I'd also add a wholesale (B2B) price 
  field per product and a "Wholesale" toggle on the billing screen, so scanning
  a tag bills the correct price automatically (the barcode already identifies
  the item).

  Here are three concrete tag layouts — pick the one you like:

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · How should the tag show the two prices (B2C retail + B2B wholesale)? → 
     Plain retail + coded wholesale

  2. Tag: show the retail ₹ in plain, readable text; the coded number now
  encodes the wholesale price (hidden). Size group stays.
  3. Product form: a wholesale-price input so staff can set it.

⏺ Draft feature built and verified — that was the last item in the queue.

  How it works:
  - While you're on New Bill, the in-progress bill auto-saves to a draft every 2
  minutes (and again the moment you navigate away), as long as it has at least
  one item.
  - Drafts are never shared — just stored. A bill you finish in under 2 minutes
  never leaves a draft.
  - When you Save & Share, that bill's draft is deleted automatically so it
  won't linger.
  - A new Drafts entry in the sidebar lists every saved draft (customer, item
  count, total, and when it was saved). Click Continue (or the name) and it
  reopens in New Bill exactly where you left off — items, customer, discount,
  tax, payment, notes.
  - Drafts are per-user.

  code-split), the draft API end-to-end (create → list → update → delete), and
  17/17 backend tests.

⏺ Done — drafts now save on every change (debounced ~0.8s so typing doesn't spam
  the server), continuously updating the same draft until you finalize with
  Save & Share (which deletes it). The Drafts page already exists from before.
  Typecheck is clean, and the list/count refresh when a draft is first created
  or finalized. Just refresh the New Bill page.

  On Redis — yes we could, but I'd keep drafts in MongoDB. Here's why:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
  │             │    MongoDB (current)     │             Redis              │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │                          │ In-memory — a restart can lose │
  │ Durability  │ Drafts survive restarts  │  drafts unless you configure   │
  │             │ automatically            │ AOF/RDB, and it's still more   │
  │             │                          │ fragile                        │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Infra       │ Already running, no new  │ A whole new server to run,     │
  │             │ service                  │ connect, deploy, and monitor   │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Fit for     │ A shop has a handful of  │ Redis's speed only matters at  │
  │ drafts      │ in-progress bills —      │ high scale you won't hit here  │
  │             │ trivial volume           │                                │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

  A draft is data you don't want to lose (that's the whole point), and the
  volume is tiny — so a durable database beats an in-memory store here. Redis
  would add a moving part and a failure mode for no real gain.

  Where Redis would actually earn its place: heavy caching, rate-limiting,
  queues, pub/sub, or session stores. The one draft-related thing it's nice for
  is auto-expiring old drafts (TTL) — but MongoDB does that too with a TTL
  index, no Redis needed.

  So my recommendation: keep drafts on Mongo (already built and working). If you
  want drafts to auto-clean after, say, 7 days, I can add a Mongo TTL index in
  a couple of lines.

  If you have a Redis instance already or want it for other features (caching,
  queues), I'm happy to wire Redis in for those — but I'd still store the drafts
  themselves in Mongo. Want me to add the 7-day auto-cleanup, or leave drafts
  permanent until deleted?

❯ [Image #15] here in the payment method - credit menas what ? credit card or

  ⎿  [Image #15]

⏺ Good question — "Credit" there is ambiguous. Let me quickly confirm how the
  backend treats it so I answer precisely:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ In this app, "Credit" means a credit sale — selling on udhaar / pay-later, not
  a credit card.

  - Card = the customer pays now by swiping a credit/debit card.
  - Credit = the customer takes the goods now and pays later; whatever's unpaid
  becomes their Balance due / outstanding on their account.

  Under the hood it's just a label — what actually decides money owed is the
  Amount paid vs Total. So for a credit sale you'd typically set Amount paid to
  0 (or a part payment), pick Credit, and the rest shows as Balance due against
  that customer.

  Since "Credit" vs "Card" trips people up, I'd suggest relabeling it to be
  unambiguous. Which do you prefer?
  - Credit (pay later)
  - Udhaar / Credit
  - Leave it as Credit

  Say the word and I'll update the label (it only changes the display text, not
  any data).

❯ I have a printer and a scanner to do physical work. As we implement, I have   
  the printer to print the labels, and I have the scanner to scan that label. I 
  want to test that. Can you please tell me how I can configure both physical   
  devices wirelessly instead of with wires?                                     

⏺ Great — this is the fun part: closing the loop between the app and real
  hardware. Good news: the app doesn't talk to the devices directly. It just
  generates a label PDF (which you print like any document) and reads keyboard 
  input from the scanner. So "wireless" is purely an OS-level pairing — once
  each device is connected to your Mac, the app works through it with zero extra
  config.

  Here's how to set up each, wirelessly.

  1. Label printer (wireless)

  Your label printer connects one of two ways — check which yours supports:

  - Wi‑Fi: Put the printer and your Mac on the same Wi‑Fi network. Use the
  printer's setup (its little screen or the manufacturer's app, e.g. the
  TSC/Zebra/TVS app) to join your Wi‑Fi. Then on the Mac: System Settings → 
  Printers & Scanners → Add Printer → it should appear → add it.
  - Bluetooth: Turn on the printer's Bluetooth, then on the Mac: System Settings
  → Bluetooth → pair it. It then shows up under Printers automatically (or Add
  Printer).

  One critical setting (this trips everyone up): set the paper/label size to
  your 50 × 25 mm label and print at actual size / 100% scale, no margins — not
  "fit to page." In the browser print dialog:
  - Paper size → your 50×25mm label (you may need to add it as a custom size in
  the printer driver first)
  - Scale → 100% (or "Actual size")
  - Margins → None

  The app's "Label printer — 50 × 25 mm" option is already sized exactly to the
  label, so at 100% it lands perfectly.

  2. Barcode scanner (wireless)

  Two common wireless types — both behave like a keyboard:

  - Bluetooth scanner: Pair it to the Mac via System Settings → Bluetooth (like
  a Bluetooth keyboard).
  - 2.4 GHz scanner: Plug its tiny USB dongle into the Mac. The
  scanner-to-dongle link is wireless; nothing to configure.

  Two scanner settings to confirm (from its manual — usually barcodes you scan
  to configure it):
  - Suffix = Enter / CR → so a scan auto-submits in the app. (Almost always the
  default.)
  - Code 128 enabled → that's the symbology our labels use. (Also usually on by
  default.)

  3. Test the full loop

  1. Print: Products → open a product → Print labels → pick Label printer — 50 ×
  25 mm → print to your wireless printer. Check the barcode is crisp and the
  label is the right size.
  2. Scan: Go to New Bill → click into the "Scan tag" box (so it's focused) →
  scan the printed label. The item should drop straight into the bill. Scan
  again → quantity goes to 2.

  That's the whole physical workflow.

  Notes

  - For the keyboard-style scanner, no HTTPS needed — it works on the current
  http://localhost:5173.
  - The in-app camera scanner (the camera button) is the only thing that needs
  HTTPS + camera permission — so for hardware testing, the wireless scanner is
  simpler.
  - Wi‑Fi printer = printer and Mac on the same network. The scanner connects to
  the Mac itself, not the network.

  If you tell me the exact make/model of your printer and scanner, I'll give you
  the precise button-by-button steps (and the right driver/label-size settings)
  for those specific devices.

      144    );

      146  
      147 +function EditShopDialog({ shop, onClose }: { shop: ShopDetails
          +; onClose: () => void }) {                                    
      148 +  const qc = useQueryClient();                                
      149 +  const [saving, setSaving] = useState(false);                
      150 +  const [form, setForm] = useState({                          
      151 +    name: shop.name ?? '',                                    
      152 +    phone: shop.phone ?? '',                                  
      153 +    email: shop.email ?? '',                                  
      154 +    gstin: shop.gstin ?? '',                                  
      155 +    state: shop.state ?? '',                                  
      156 +    stateCode: shop.stateCode ?? '',                          
      157 +    defaultHsn: shop.defaultHsn ?? '',                        
      158 +    address: shop.address ?? '',                              
      159 +    bankName: shop.bank?.name ?? '',                          
      160 +    bankAccount: shop.bank?.account ?? '',                    
      161 +    bankIfsc: shop.bank?.ifsc ?? '',                          
      162 +  });                                                         
      163 +  const set = (k: keyof typeof form) => (e: React.ChangeEvent<
          +HTMLInputElement>) => setForm({ ...form, [k]: e.target.value }
          +);                                                            
      164 +                                                              
      165 +  const save = async () => {                                  
      166 +    if (!form.name.trim()) return toast.error('Shop name is re
          +quired');                                                     
      167 +    setSaving(true);                                          
      168 +    try {                                                     
      169 +      await api.put('/settings/shop', {                       
      170 +        name: form.name.trim(),                               
      171 +        phone: form.phone.trim(),                             
      172 +        email: form.email.trim(),                             
      173 +        gstin: form.gstin.trim(),                             
      174 +        state: form.state.trim(),                             
      175 +        stateCode: form.stateCode.trim(),                     
      176 +        defaultHsn: form.defaultHsn.trim(),                   
      177 +        address: form.address.trim(),                         
      178 +        bank: { name: form.bankName.trim(), account: form.bank
          +Account.trim(), ifsc: form.bankIfsc.trim() },                 
      179 +      });                                                     
      180 +      toast.success('Shop details updated');                  
      181 +      qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ['shop-details'] });   
      182 +      onClose();                                              
      183 +    } catch (err) {                                           
      184 +      toast.error(getApiError(err).message);                  
      185 +    } finally {                                               
      186 +      setSaving(false);                                       
      187 +    }                                                         
      188 +  };                                                          
      189 +                                                              
      190 +  return (                                                    
      191 +    <Dialog open onOpenChange={(v) => !v && onClose()}>       
      192 +      <DialogContent className="max-w-xl">                    
      193 +        <DialogHeader><DialogTitle>Edit shop details</DialogTi
          +tle></DialogHeader>                                           
      194 +        <div className="grid max-h-[65vh] gap-3 overflow-y-aut
          +o pr-1 sm:grid-cols-2">                                       
      195 +          <div className="sm:col-span-2"><Label>Shop name *</L

⏺ Done and verified. Downloaded bill PDFs are now named
  customer_bill-no_date_time — the live header confirms it:

  Rushikesh-Malpani_INV-000019_20-10-2026_13-30.pdf

  Format: {Customer name}_{Invoice no}_{DD-MM-YYYY}_{HH-mm}.pdf (spaces in the
  name become hyphens; walk-in customers show "Walk-in").

  How it works:
  - The backend builds the filename from the invoice (customer snapshot, number,
  and date/time) and sends it in the download header.
  - The frontend reads that header, so the Download button and the
  WhatsApp-shared PDF file both get the same rich name (previously just
  INV-000019.pdf).
  - I also exposed the Content-Disposition header via CORS so the browser can
  read it.

  Both sides typecheck clean and the backend reloaded — download a bill and
  you'll see the new filename.

  change that made b2bPrice/b2cPrice required on products — unrelated to this.

      139 -            <div className="max-h-[60vh] space-y-3 overflow-y-
          -auto pr-1">                                                   
      139 +            <div className="max-h-[60vh] space-y-3 overflow-y-
          +auto p-1.5">                                                  
      140                <div>
      141                  <Label>Full name *</Label>
      142                  <Input value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e
           .target.value)} placeholder="Customer name" autoFocus />

⏺ Fixed. The scroll area now has padding on all sides (p-1.5), so the focus
  outline sits inside the dialog instead of spilling over the left edge. Applied
  to both the Edit shop details dialog and the Add customer form (same
  pattern).
